. Comparison of the finite-length achievability bounds from Theorem
1
(evaluated for
L=100
),
the asymptotic achievability bounds from Theorem
2
with
\Lambda\to\infty
then
\omega\to\infty
, and the asymptotic performance characterization of the CDMA scheme in Theorem
4
with specific
(\omega,\Lambda)
.

Fig.
12b
considers a larger user payload
k=60
. The asymptotic achievability bounds are computed using
\mathcal{F}_{\textrm{marginal}}
because those based on
\mathcal{F}_{\textrm{Bayes}}
are computationally infeasible for such a large
k
.
The CDMA schemes employ the thresholding denoiser
\eta_{t}^{\textrm{thres}}
or the marginal-MMSE denoiser
\eta_{t}^{\textrm{marginal}}
because the Bayes-optimal denoiser
\eta_{t}^{\textrm{Bayes}}
is infeasible. We observe that
\eta_{t}^{\textrm{thres}}
significantly outperforms
\eta_{t}^{\textrm{marginal}}
since it takes into account the correlation between the
k
bipolar symbols transmitted by each user.
Note that at
\mu_{\mathrm{a}}=0.013
, the transition thresholds of the CDMA curves (i.e., 8dB and 14.5dB) in Fig.
12b
are consistent with the
E_{b}/N_{0}
values where the curves plateau in Fig.
10
.

Finally, the dotted black curve in Fig.
12b
plots the finite-length achievability bound from Theorem
1
using
L=100,{r_{l}}={r_{\mathrm{u}}}=L,

P^{\prime}
optimized over
(0,P)
, and
({\kappa_{l}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}})
chosen so that
\mathbb{P}({K_{\mathrm{a}}}\notin[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}])\leq 10^{-6}
. The curve exhibits a slope similar to the asymptotic achievability bound based on
\mathcal{F}_{\textrm{marginal}}
(solid black curve). Notably, for
\mu_{\mathrm{a}}\leq 0.026
or
\mu_{\mathrm{a}}\geq 0.057
, the finite-length bound achieves the target error metric over a larger range of
E_{b}/N_{0}
values than the bounds for the efficient CDMA schemes (orange and green curves). This confirms that random coding with maximum-likelihood decoding outperforms CDMA with suboptimal AMP decoding as expected.

5
Proof of Theorem
1

5.1
Preliminaries

For unsourced multiple-access with random user activity, where all users share the same codebook, Ngo et al. recently obtained a finite-length achievability bound
[
11
, Theorem 1]
.
We follow the structure of their proof and adapt their bounds to the standard (sourced) GMAC where users have distinct codebooks. As in
[
3
,
11
]
, the proof of Theorem
1
involves union bounds over error events via a change of measure, Chernoff bound and Gallager’s
\rho
-trick, which we lay out as preliminaries below.
A key additional technical step in our setting is to carefully separate error events into the three categories defined in (
3
),(
4
), and (
5
).

The proof, except for the final step, assumes a given Gaussian joint-codebook from the ensemble described in Section
2.1
and establishes the error probability guarantees in (
8
)–(
10
) individually. The final step then shows the existence of a randomized coding scheme that uses time-sharing among two such joint-codebooks to simultaneously achieve the bounds (
8
)–(
10
).

Lemma 2

(Change of measure,
[
56
, Lemma 4]
)
.

Let
p
and
q
be two probability measures. Consider a random variable
X
supported on a set
\mathcal{S}
and a function
f:\mathcal{S}\to[0,1].
It holds that
\mathbb{E}_{p}[f(X)]\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}[f(X)]+d_{\mathrm{TV}}(p,q)
, where
d_{\mathrm{TV}}(p,q)
denotes the total variation distance between
p
and
q
, i.e.,
d_{\mathrm{TV}}(p,q)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{S}}\left\lvert\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{\mathrm{d}q}-1\right\rvert\mathrm{d}q
.

Lemma 3

(Chernoff bound)
.

For a random variable
X
with moment-generating function
\mathbb{E}[e^{tX}]
defined for all
\left\lvert t\right\rvert\leq b
, it holds for all
\lambda\in[0,b]
that
\mathbb{P}(X\leq x)\leq e^{\lambda x}\mathbb{E}[e^{-\lambda X}]
.

Lemma 4

(Gallager’s
\rho
-trick)
.

For a series of events
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{m}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\cup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{i})\right)^{\rho}\quad\forall\rho\in[0,1].

(110)

Moreover, if
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{i}|V)\leq e^{-E(V)}
for
i\in[m]
, where
V
is some random variable and
E(V)
a function of
V
, then (
110
) implies that

\mathbb{P}(\cup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{i})\leq m^{\rho}\,\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[e^{-\rho E(V)}\right].

(111)

Lemma 5

(Gaussian identity)
.

For
\boldsymbol{X}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\mu},\sigma^{2}\boldsymbol{I})
,

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\gamma\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{2}^{2}}\right]=(1+2\sigma^{2}\gamma)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma\|\boldsymbol{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2\sigma^{2}\gamma}\right),\quad\forall\gamma>-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}.

Change of measure

Recall the definitions of the error probabilities
p_{\mathrm{MD}},p_{\mathrm{FA}}
and
p_{\mathrm{AUE}}
from (
3
)–(
5
).
Since the quantities inside the expectations in (
3
)–(
5
) are bounded between 0 and 1, using Lemma
2
, we replace the measure over which the expectations are taken with a new measure, denoted by
q
, under which: i)
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
almost surely, and ii) the codeword transmitted by user
\ell
is simply
\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}
instead of
\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\mathbbm{1}\{\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq E_{b}k\}
for every
(\ell,w_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}
. Recall that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},E_{b}^{\prime}k/n\boldsymbol{I})
and
P^{\prime}=E_{b}^{\prime}k/n<P=E_{b}k/n
. Thus, recalling
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}:=|\mathcal{W}|
, the total variation distance
d_{\mathrm{TV}}
between the new and original measures is upper bounded by

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}({K_{\mathrm{a}}}\notin[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}])+\mathbb{E}_{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\ell:(\ell,w_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}}\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}>E_{b}k\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}({K_{\mathrm{a}}}\notin[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}])+\mathbb{E}_{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\left[\sum_{\ell:(\ell,w_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}>nP\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}({K_{\mathrm{a}}}\notin[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}])+\mathbb{E}[{K_{\mathrm{a}}}]\frac{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2},\frac{nP}{2P^{\prime}})}{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})}=:\tilde{p}\,,

(112)

where (a) applies a union bound and uses

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}>E_{b}k\,\big|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}>E_{b}k\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}>nP\right),

and (b) holds because
\frac{1}{P^{\prime}}\|\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{\ell}}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}^{2}\sim\textrm{Gamma}(\frac{n}{2},2)
and
\mathbb{P}\left(\textrm{Gamma}(n^{\prime},\theta)>z_{0}\right)=\Gamma(n^{\prime},\frac{z_{0}}{\theta})/\Gamma(n^{\prime})
.
Therefore, Lemma
2
implies that

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{MD}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\neq 0\}\cdot\frac{1}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\sum_{\ell:(\ell,w_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbbm{1}\{\widehat{w_{\ell}}=\emptyset\}\right]+\tilde{p}\,,

(113)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{FA}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\neq 0\}\cdot\frac{1}{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}\sum_{\ell:(\ell,\widehat{w}_{\ell})\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}}\mathbbm{1}\{w_{\ell}=\emptyset\}\right]+\tilde{p}\,,

(114)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{AUE}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\neq 0\}\cdot\frac{1}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\sum_{\ell:(\ell,w_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbbm{1}\big\{\widehat{w_{\ell}}\notin\{w_{\ell},\emptyset\}\big\}\right]+\tilde{p}\,.

(115)

In the next subsection, we evaluate the
\mathbb{E}_{q}[\cdot]
terms on the right side in a simple special case, with the calculations for the general case deferred to Section
5.3
.

5.2
A Special Case

Under the new measure
q
, we begin by analyzing the error probabilities for a special case where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are both assumed fixed,
{r_{l}}={r_{\mathrm{u}}}=0
and
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\leq L
.
As a result,
\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}=\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}:=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
in (
7
).

The Venn diagram in Fig.
13
illustrates the relation between different sets of codewords in this special case. The intersection
\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
denotes the set of transmitted codewords that are decoded correctly.
The set in gray,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
, is called the
initial
set of
\mathtt{MD}
s, which represents the
({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})
transmitted codewords that are guaranteed to be
missed
simply because
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
.

In addition to
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
, the remaining sets
(\mathcal{W}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}=:\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}\setminus\mathcal{W}=:\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
represent the
extra
codewords in error. The sets in blue,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
, represent the extra
\mathtt{MD}
s and
\mathtt{FA}
s. Their elements have a one-to-one correspondence because each active user declared silent corresponds to a silent user declared active. Therefore these sets share the same size
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|
.

Figure 13:
Venn diagram illustrating the relation between different sets of messages in the special case defined in Section
5.2
, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are fixed,
{r_{l}}={r_{\mathrm{u}}}=0
and
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
, and integers
t\in[0:{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
and
\psi\in[0:\min\{t,L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\}]
. Diagram analogous to
[
11
, Fig. 7]
.

The sets in orange,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
, represent the extra active user errors (
\mathtt{AUE}
s), where the users who transmitted the codewords
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
are declared active, but their codewords are mistaken for some other codewords
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
in their respective codebooks. Thus, these sets also have the same size
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}\right\rvert=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|
. Noting that
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
, we have
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|
.
In terms of notation, the subscripts “i” and “e” denote, respectively, the initial errors due to
{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
being different from
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
, and the extra errors occurred in the further decoding process.

Based on the relation between different sets of messages illustrated in Fig.
13
, and the fact that
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are fixed in the special case, we simplify the right side of (
113
)–(
115
) as follows:

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{MD}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\frac{\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}=\frac{({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})+\mathbb{E}_{q}\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert}{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}}+\tilde{p},

(116)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{FA}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}\right]+\tilde{p}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q}|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|}{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}+\tilde{p},

(117)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{AUE}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert-\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert\right]}{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}}+\tilde{p}.

(118)

Note that
\mathbb{E}_{q}\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert=\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\mathbb{E}_{q}\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert\big|\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert\right]
by the law of total expectation.
Therefore, to evaluate the right side of (
116
)–(
118
), we only need to characterize the marginal distribution of
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert
, and the distribution of
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert
conditioned on
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert
. We first provide these distributions in Lemmas
6
and
7
, and then prove the lemmas. For simplicity, we will henceforth drop the subscript
q
from
\mathbb{E}_{q}[\cdot]
, and stress that all expectations are with respect to the new measure
q
.

Lemma 6
.

In the special case, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
and
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are fixed,
{r_{l}}={r_{\mathrm{u}}}=0
and

{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\leq L
, it holds for every integer
t\in[0:{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\check{E}(t)\right),

(119)

where

\displaystyle\check{E}(t)=\max_{\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1]}\big[-\rho\rho_{1}\check{R}_{1}(t)-\rho_{1}\check{R}_{2}(t)+\check{E}_{0}(\rho,\rho_{1})\big],

(120)

\displaystyle\check{R}_{1}(t)=\frac{2}{n}\left(t\ln M+\ln\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}\right),\quad\check{\mathcal{R}}=L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}+t,\quad\check{R}_{2}(t)=\frac{2}{n}\ln\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t},

(121)

\displaystyle\check{E}_{0}(\rho,\rho_{1})=\max_{\check{\lambda}>-\frac{1}{P^{\prime}t}}\big[\rho_{1}\check{a}(\rho,\check{\lambda})+\ln(1-\rho_{1}\check{P}_{1}\check{b}(\rho,\check{\lambda}))\big],

(122)

\displaystyle\check{a}(\rho,\check{\lambda})=\rho\ln(1+P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda})+\ln\left(1+P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}(\rho,\check{\lambda})\right),

(123)

\displaystyle\check{b}(\rho,\check{\lambda})=\rho\check{\lambda}-\frac{\check{\chi}(\rho,\check{\lambda})}{1+P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}(\rho,\check{\lambda})},\qquad\check{\chi}(\rho,\check{\lambda})=\frac{\rho\check{\lambda}}{1+P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}},

(124)

\displaystyle\check{P}_{1}=({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})P^{\prime}+1\,.

(125)

Lemma 7
.

Consider the special case, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
and
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are fixed,
{r_{l}}={r_{\mathrm{u}}}=0
and

{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\leq L
. Define
\check{\mathcal{R}}=L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}+t
. For every integer
t\in[0:{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
and
\psi\in[0:\min\{t,\check{\mathcal{R}}-t\}]
, let

\displaystyle\nu(t,\psi)=\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}-t}{\psi}M^{\psi}\cdot\binom{t}{t-\psi}(M-1)^{t-\psi},\quad\nu(t)=\sum_{\psi=0}^{\min\{t,\check{\mathcal{R}}-t\}}\nu(t,\psi).

(126)

Then the following hold:

1.

Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
, the number of distinct choices for the set
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
equals
\nu(t)
. Furthermore,
\nu(t)\leq\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}M^{t}
.

2.

It holds that
\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|=\psi\,\bigg|\,|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t\right)=\nu(t,\psi)/\nu(t)
.

Substituting (
119
)–(
126
) into (
116
)–(
118
) yields Theorem
1
for the special case. We next provide the proofs of Lemmas
6
and
7
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6
.

We recall from (
7
) the notation
c(\mathcal{W}^{\prime}):=\sum_{j:(j,w_{j}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{W}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{c}_{w_{j}^{\prime}}^{(j)}\,
. The channel output takes the form

\boldsymbol{y}=c(\mathcal{W})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})+c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\,,

(127)

where we have used the fact
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\,\cup\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\,\cup\,(W\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})
,
where the subsets forming the union are pairwise disjoint
(see Fig.
13
).
Since the set decoded by the maximum-likelihood decoder can be expressed as
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}\cup(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{y}-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\|_{2}^{2}\,<\,\|\boldsymbol{y}-c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\|_{2}^{2}\,,

where we have used
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|
.
Combined with
(
127
), this is equivalent to

\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}<\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(128)

Letting
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})
denote the event that
(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})
satisfies (
128
) with
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t}\,\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\right),\quad\text{for}\;t\in[0:{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}].

(129)

To bound the right side of (
129
), we first note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}<\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{\leq}}\exp\left(\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}}\left[\exp\left(-\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\exp\left(\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0}}\right),\;\forall\check{\lambda}_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}t}

\displaystyle=:\exp\left(-\textsf{A}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\right),

(130)

where (a) applies the Chernoff bound in Lemma
3
, and (b) applies the Gaussian identity in Lemma
5
, noting that
c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},P^{\prime}t\boldsymbol{I})
.
We now apply Gallager’s
\rho
-trick (
111
) twice to add two unions
\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}}
and
\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}}
to the left side of (
130
) and obtain an upper bound on (
129
).

\rho
-trick for
\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}}

We first apply (
111
) to add the union over
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
to the left side of (
130
), obtaining for every
\rho\in[0,1],

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\left[\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}M^{t}\right]^{\rho}\exp\left(-\rho\textsf{A}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\right),

(131)

where recall that
M
denotes the number of messages in each codebook.
Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
, the multiplicative factor
\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}M^{t}

is an upper bound on the number of ways to construct
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
given
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
, as we prove in Lemma
7
, Part
1
.
Expanding the right side of (
131
)
and taking expectation over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho}M^{\rho t}(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0})^{-\frac{n\rho}{2}}\exp\left(\rho\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\;\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{\rho\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0}}\right)\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho}M^{\rho t}(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0})^{-\frac{n\rho}{2}}\exp\left(\rho\check{\lambda}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\;\;\left[(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}_{0})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\check{\chi}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}_{0}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho}M^{\rho t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\check{a}_{0}}{2}+\check{b}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right),

(132)

where (a) applies the Gaussian identity in Lemma
5
and the shorthand
\check{\chi}_{0}:=\rho\check{\lambda}_{0}/(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0})
, noting that
c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},P^{\prime}t\boldsymbol{I})
; (b) uses the
shorthand
\check{b}_{0}:=\rho\check{\lambda}_{0}-{\check{\chi}_{0}}/(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}_{0})
and
\check{a}_{0}:=\rho\ln(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\lambda}_{0})+\ln(1+2P^{\prime}t\check{\chi}_{0})
.

\rho
-trick for
\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}}

We now apply the
\rho
-trick (
111
) again to add the union over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
to the left side of (
132
).
Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
, since
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\subset\mathcal{W}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
and
|\mathcal{W}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}|={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, there are
\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}
ways to construct
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
t
. Therefore, for
\rho_{1}\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}\check{a}_{0}}{2}+\rho_{1}\check{b}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,.

(133)

Taking expectation over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{I})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}\check{a}_{0}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\exp\left(\rho_{1}\check{b}_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}\check{a}_{0}}{2}\right)\left[1-2\rho_{1}\check{P}_{1}\check{b}_{0}\right]^{-\frac{n}{2}}.

(134)

The last step in (
134
) applies the Gaussian identity in Lemma
5
to
c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},\check{P}_{1}\boldsymbol{I})
with
\check{P}_{1}=\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\right\rvert P^{\prime}+1=({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})P^{\prime}+1
as defined in (
125
).

Finally, substituting (
134
) into (
129
)
and minimizing the upper bound with respect to
\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1]
and
\check{\lambda}_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}t}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t\right)\leq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1],\\
\check{\lambda}_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}t}\end{subarray}}\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}\check{a}_{0}}{2}\right)\left[1-2\rho_{1}\check{P}_{1}\check{b}_{0}\right]^{-\frac{n}{2}}

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1],\\
\check{\lambda}>-\frac{1}{P^{\prime}t}\end{subarray}}\binom{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}t}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}\check{a}}{2}\right)\left[1-\rho_{1}\check{P}_{1}\check{b}\right]^{-\frac{n}{2}}=\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\check{E}(t)\right),

(135)

where
\check{\lambda}:=2\check{\lambda}_{0}
,
\check{E}(t)
is given in (
120
)–(
122
), and
\check{a},\check{b},\check{\chi}
are given in (
123
)–(
124
), which implies that
\check{a}_{0}=\check{a}
,
\check{b}_{0}=\check{b}/2
and
\check{\chi}_{0}=\check{\chi}/2
.
∎

Remark 2

(Comparisons with unsourced setting)
.

For unsourced random access, the
\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}M^{t}
factor in (
131
) needs to be replaced by
\binom{M-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}}{t}
, as in
[
11
, Eq. (82)]
. This is because in unsourced random access, the
t
elements of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
are chosen from the
(M-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})
codewords that are not transmitted in the common codebook.

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Proof of part
1
:

Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
,
we observe from Fig.
13
that each element of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
comes from either:

1.

one of the codebooks of the
L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}=\check{\mathcal{R}}-t
silent users, or

2.

the set of untransmitted codewords in one of the codebooks of the
t
users who transmitted
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
.

We refer to these sets as type-
1
and type-
2
, respectively.
Noting that
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
, each element of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
comes from a type-
1
codebook and each element of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
comes from a type-
2
codebook excluding
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
. The decoding rule (
7
) ensures that all the elements of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
come from distinct codebooks.
Hence, to have
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
with
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|=\psi
and
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|=t-\psi
, for
\psi\in[0:\min\{t,L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\}]
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
needs to contain
\psi
codewords, each from a distinct type-
1
codebook, and
(t-\psi)
codewords, each from a distinct type-
2
codebook. There are

\nu(t,\psi)=\binom{L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}}{\psi}M^{\psi}\cdot\binom{t}{t-\psi}(M-1)^{t-\psi}

(136)

ways to construct such a set
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
.
Moreover, recalling
\check{\mathcal{R}}=L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}+t
,
there are

\displaystyle\nu(t)\coloneqq\sum_{\psi=0}^{\min\{t,L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\}}\nu(t,\psi)\leq\sum_{\psi=0}^{\min\{t,L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\}}\binom{L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}}{\psi}\cdot\binom{t}{t-\psi}\cdot M^{t}=\binom{\check{\mathcal{R}}}{t}M^{t}

(137)

ways in total to construct
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
for any valid values of
\psi
,
where the inequality in (
137
) holds due to Vandermonde’s identity.

Proof of part
2
:

We characterize the conditional distribution of
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|

by characterizing the conditional distribution of
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|
since
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|
(see Fig.
13
).
The key is to leverage the combinatorial argument established in part
1
. Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
, we know there are
\nu(t,\psi)
possible configurations for
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
with size
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|=\psi
, and
\nu(t)
total possible configurations for
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
.

Since all codewords are exchangeable across codebooks, each possible configuration of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
occurs with equal probability.
We therefore have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|=\psi\,\bigg|\,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right)=\frac{\nu(t,\psi)}{\nu(t)}\,.

(138)

Finally, using
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|
and
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|

and taking expectation over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
concludes the proof.

∎

5.3
The General Case

Figure 14:
Venn diagram illustrating the relation between different sets of messages in the general case, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
are random and
{r_{l}},{r_{\mathrm{u}}}\geq 0
. Diagram analogous to
[
11
, Fig. 8]
. Recall notation
x^{+}=\max\{x,0\}
.

This section provides the proof of Theorem
1
in the general case, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
(and hence
{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}
) are random, and the decoding
radii
{r_{l}},{r_{\mathrm{u}}}\geq 0
. As before, we use
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
to denote the realization of random variables
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\underline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\overline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}
.

Fig.
14
illustrates the relation between different sets of codewords.
As before, let
\mathcal{W}
be the set of transmitted codewords with size
\left\lvert\mathcal{W}\right\rvert={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
in a particular instance.
Recall that given
{r_{l}}
and
{r_{\mathrm{u}}}
, the decoder computes
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
in (
6
) and determines the decoded set
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
in (
7
) with size
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|=\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
, where
\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are functions of
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
.
Similar to the special case, the difference between
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
and
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
leads to
|{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}|

\mathtt{MD}
s or
\mathtt{FA}
s, depending on whether or not
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
.
However, given
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
and
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
(hence
\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
),
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
is unknown and the only knowledge we have about
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
is that
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
. Therefore,

1.

If
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, we are guaranteed with
({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})

\mathtt{MD}
s initially, represented by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
.

2.

If
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}<\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, we are guaranteed with
(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})

\mathtt{FA}
s initially, represented by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}
.

3.

If
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
, the relation between
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
and
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
is unknown, so we cannot declare any
\mathtt{MD}
s or
\mathtt{FA}
s initially.

To ensure our results hold across all three scenarios
1
,
2
and
3
, we denote
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}|
by
({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}
and
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}|
by
(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}
. The
\{\cdot\}^{+}
operators ensure that at most one of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}
is non-empty.

As before,
\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
represents the set of transmitted codewords decoded correctly. The sets

\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:=(\mathcal{W}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
and

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:=(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}\setminus\mathcal{W})\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}
correspond to any additional errors.
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
contains the extra
\mathtt{MD}
s and
\mathtt{AUE}
s, while
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
contains the extra
\mathtt{FA}
s and
\mathtt{AUE}
s.
In contrast to the special case where
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|
, in the general case
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
may differ in size. We denote their sizes by
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
and
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}
.
It follows that there are
|t-{\hat{t}}|
additional
\mathtt{MD}
s or
\mathtt{FA}
s depending on whether or not
t>{\hat{t}}
. These extra errors are represented by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
, respectively, with sizes
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|=(t-{\hat{t}})^{+}
and
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|=({\hat{t}}-t)^{+}
.

Having defined
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}},
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}},
it is easy to see that the remaining sets
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
share the same size
{t_{\min}}:=\min\{t,{\hat{t}}\}
.
Similar to the special case,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
each contains two types of errors, coloured in blue and orange, respectively.
The sets in blue, denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}^{1}
, are the extra
\mathtt{MD}
s and
\mathtt{FA}
s. Their elements
are in one-to-one correspondence with
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}^{1}|
: each transmitted codeword
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}
corresponds to a unique decoded codeword
\widehat{w}\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}^{1}
from a silent user’s codebook, where
w
is mistakenly decoded as
\widehat{w}
.

The sets in orange, denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
, are the extra
\mathtt{AUE}
s. Their elements
are also in one-to-one correspondence with
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|
, because for every transmitted codeword
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
, there is a unique decoded codeword
\widehat{w}\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}
from the same codebook as
w
such that
w
is decoded as
\widehat{w}
.

(a)
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
implies
t\geq{\hat{t}}
.

(b)
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}<\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
implies
t\leq{\hat{t}}
.

(c)
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
allows either
t\geq{\hat{t}}
or
t<{\hat{t}}
.

Figure 15:
Venn diagrams illustrating the relation between different sets of messages in the general case, where
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
are random and
{r_{l}},{r_{\mathrm{u}}}\geq 0
. Subfigures correspond to three different scenarios:
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
,
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}<\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, or
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
.

Remark 3

(Different values of
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
)
.

Note that
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
implies
t\geq{\hat{t}}
, and

{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}<\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
implies
t\leq{\hat{t}}
, whereas
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
allows
t
and
{\hat{t}}
to have either relation. We verify the first claim as an example: when
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\geq\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
, we have
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}=|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}|+|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|
, which implies that
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}|=({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})-|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}|=\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}=|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\cup(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})|-|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}\cup(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})|=t-{\hat{t}}\geq 0
(see Fig.
15a
).
The second and third
claims can be verified similarly.
Fig.
15
illustrates how the relation between different sets of messages shown in Fig.
14
specializes to the three different cases:
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}>\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
,
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}<\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, or
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
.

We now proceed to prove Theorem
1
in the general case. First note that the right side of (
113
)–(
115
) can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{MD}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}\right\rvert}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{({K_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}+(|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|-|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|)^{+}+\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}\right\rvert}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}\,,

(139)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{FA}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}|+|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}|+|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}^{1}|}{{\widehat{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\underline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{K_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}+(|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|-|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|)^{+}+|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}|}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}-|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|-({K_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}+|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|+(\underline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{K_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}}\right]+\tilde{p}\,,

(140)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{AUE}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eAUE}}|}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\min\{|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|\}-|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}|}{{K_{\mathrm{a}}}}\right]+\tilde{p}\,.

(141)

Since
\underline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
are functions of
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, the expectations above are w.r.t.
({K_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|)
. To evaluate the right side of (
139
)–(
141
), observe that it suffices to characterize the joint distribution of
({K_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|)
and the distribution of
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}|
conditioned on
({K_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|)
. We provide these distributions in Lemmas
8

and
9
after introducing the necessary notation.

Following the definitions in (
12
)–(
13
) of Theorem
1
, let

{\mathcal{T}}
denote the set of possible values for
t=|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|
, and let
{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{t}}
denote the set of possible values for
{\hat{t}}=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|
when
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
.

We repeat these definitions below for convenience:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}:=\left[0:\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}\right],

(142)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{t}}:=\left[\left\{t+({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}-({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}\right\}^{+}:{t_{\mathrm{u}}}\right],

(143)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad{t_{\mathrm{u}}}:=\min\left\{\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+},\;t+(\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}-(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}\right\}.

(144)

In particular,
Eq. (
142
)
follows from
|\mathcal{W}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|=|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|\leq|\mathcal{W}|
, i.e.,
({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}+t\leq{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
. We verify (
143
)–(
144
) by noticing that (i)
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}\setminus\mathcal{W}|=|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|\leq|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|=\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\leq\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
leads to
(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}+{\hat{t}}\leq\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, and (ii)
\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
implies

\displaystyle\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\leq\widehat{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-t-({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}+{\hat{t}}+(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}\leq\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{\hat{t}}\leq t+(\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}-(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}\\
{\hat{t}}\geq t+({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}-({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}\,.\end{cases}

We next present Lemmas
8
and
9
using the definitions in (
142
)–(
144
). We also recall
that

{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
under the new measure specified in Section
5.1
, and that
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
due to the constraints in the decoding rule (
6
).

Lemma 8
.

In the general case, for every
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
,
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
,
t\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\hat{t}}\in{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{t}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}\right)\leq p_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\left({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)\min\left\{p(t,{\hat{t}}),\xi({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})\right\},

(145)

where
p(t,{\hat{t}})
and
\xi({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})
are defined in (
14
) and (
22
) of Theorem
1
.

Lemma 9
.

In the general case, define
{t_{\min}}:=\min\{t,{\hat{t}}\}
,
\mathcal{R}:=L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}+{t_{\min}}-(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}-({\hat{t}}-t)^{+}\geq{t_{\min}}
, and
{\psi_{\mathrm{u}}}:=\min\{{t_{\min}},\mathcal{R}-{t_{\min}}\}
as in (
16
) and (
25
) of Theorem
1
. For every
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
,
{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]
,
t\in{\mathcal{T}}
,
{\hat{t}}\in{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{t}}
, and
\psi\in[0:{\psi_{\mathrm{u}}}]
, let

\displaystyle\nu({t_{\min}},\psi)=\binom{\mathcal{R}-{t_{\min}}}{\psi}M^{\psi}\cdot\binom{{t_{\min}}}{{t_{\min}}-\psi}(M-1)^{{t_{\min}}-\psi},\quad\nu({t_{\min}})=\sum_{\psi=0}^{{\psi_{\mathrm{u}}}}\nu({t_{\min}},\psi).

(146)

Then the following hold:

1.

Given
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
(with
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
),
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}
,
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
, the number of distinct choices for the set
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
equals
\nu({t_{\min}})
. Furthermore,
\nu({t_{\min}})\leq\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}M^{t_{\min}}
.

2.

It holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}^{1}|=\psi\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}\right)=\nu({t_{\min}},\psi)/\nu({t_{\min}}).

(147)

For the given Gaussian joint-codebook (described in Section
5.1
)
, substituting (
145
)–(
146
) into (
139
)–(
141
)

establishes the individual guarantees in
(
8
)–(
10
) of Theorem
1
.
To show that (
8
)–(
10
) hold simultaneously, we proceed similarly to
[
46
, Theorem 19]
and
[
10
, Theorem 8]
by constructing a randomized coding scheme that uses time-sharing among at most two joint-codebooks from the Gaussian ensemble. This completes the proof of Theorem
1
in the general case.

In the remainder of this section, we provide the proofs of Lemmas
8
and
9
, which are analogous to the proofs of Lemmas
6
and
7
for the special case.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

To prove (
145
), by the law of total probability
we need to show

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)\leq\min\{p(t,{\hat{t}}),\xi({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})\}.

(148)

As before, let
\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
denote the realizations of
\underline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
when
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
, given
{\kappa_{l}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}
,
{r_{l}}
and
{r_{\mathrm{u}}}
.
From the decoding rule in
(
6
)–(
7
), we have with
\boldsymbol{y}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}P^{\prime}+1)\boldsymbol{I})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t,\,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\argmax_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]}\,p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\kappa)={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}},\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}},|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min\Bigg\{\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\argmax_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]}\,p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\kappa)={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\right)}_{\text{term (i)}},\;\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\right\rvert=t,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}},|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]\,\bigg|\,{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}\right)}_{\text{term (ii)}}\Bigg\}\,,

(149)

where the first step holds because
{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}
implies
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
, and
the second step holds because
\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)\leq\min\{\mathbb{P}(A),\mathbb{P}(B)\}
.

We bound the terms (i) and (ii) on the right side of (
149
). For the first term, using
\boldsymbol{y}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{n}(\boldsymbol{0},({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}P^{\prime}+1)\boldsymbol{I})
, we have

term (i)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})>p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\kappa),\,\forall\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]\setminus{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]\setminus{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\left(p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})>p(\boldsymbol{y}|{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\kappa)\right)

\displaystyle=\min_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]\setminus{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{-\frac{n}{2}\ln(1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime})-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}_{2}}{2(1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime})}>-\frac{n}{2}\ln(1+\kappa P^{\prime})-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}_{2}}{2(1+\kappa P^{\prime})}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\min_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]\setminus{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{1+\kappa P^{\prime}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{y}\|_{2}^{2}<n\ln\left(\frac{1+\kappa P^{\prime}}{1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\min_{\kappa\in[{\kappa_{l}}:{\kappa_{\mathrm{u}}}]\setminus{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}}\Bigg\{\mathbbm{1}\{\kappa<{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2},\zeta)}{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})}+\mathbbm{1}\{\kappa>{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}\frac{\gamma(\frac{n}{2},\zeta)}{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})}\Bigg\}=\xi({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})\,,

(150)

where (a) uses

\frac{1}{1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}P^{\prime}}\|\boldsymbol{y}\|_{2}^{2}\sim\text{Gamma}(\frac{n}{2},2)
and

\displaystyle\zeta=\frac{n}{2(1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}P^{\prime})}\ln\left(\frac{1+\kappa P^{\prime}}{1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime}}\right)\left[\frac{1}{1+{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}P^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{1+\kappa P^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}.

(151)

Results (
150
)–(
151
) correspond to (
22
)–(
23
) in Theorem
1
.

Next, we bound term (ii) in (
149
). Similar to the special case, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}=c(\mathcal{W})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})+c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

(152)

The decoder returns
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}\cup(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})
as its estimate, which implies that

\|\boldsymbol{y}-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\|_{2}^{2}<\|\boldsymbol{y}-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})-c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\mathcal{W}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{W}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Combined with
(
152
), this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}<\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}.

(153)

Let
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})
be the event that
(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})
satisfies (
153
)
with
{K_{\mathrm{a}}}={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
,
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}
and
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}|\in[\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}:\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}]
. Then we can rewrite term (ii) in (
149
) as

\displaystyle\text{term (ii)}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\right\}.

(154)

We follow the same steps as in (
129
)–(
135
) to bound (
154
). First note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{\leq}}\exp\left(\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}}\left[\exp\left(-\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\exp\left(\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad(1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0}}\right),\;\forall\lambda_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}}\,,

(155)

where (a) applies the Chernoff bound in Lemma
3
, and (b) applies the Gaussian identity in Lemma
5
.

Define the shorthand
\chi_{0}=\rho\lambda_{0}/(1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0}),b_{0}=\rho\lambda_{0}-\chi_{0}/(1+2P^{\prime}t\chi_{0})
and
a_{0}=\rho\ln\left(1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0}\right)+\ln\left(1+2P^{\prime}t\chi_{0}\right)
. Analogous to (
131
)–(
134
) in the special case, next we add two unions over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
to the event in (
155
) and use Gallager’s
\rho
-trick (
111
) twice to obtain an upper bound on (
154
).

\rho
-trick for
\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}}

We apply the
\rho
-trick (
111
) to add a union over
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
to the left side of (
155
).
As we prove in Lemma
9
part
1
,
the number of ways to construct
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}
is upper bounded by
\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}M^{t_{\min}}
, where
{t_{\min}}=\min\{t,{\hat{t}}\}
and

\mathcal{R}=L-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}+{t_{\min}}-(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+}-({\hat{t}}-t)^{+}\geq{t_{\min}}
.

Hence applying the
\rho
-trick
and taking expectation over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{eMD}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{eFA}}
yields for
\rho\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}M^{t_{\min}}\right]^{\rho}(1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0})^{-\frac{n\rho}{2}}\exp\left(\rho\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}}\left[\exp\left(-\chi_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})+c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,\bigg|\,\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}^{\rho}M^{\rho{t_{\min}}}(1+2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}\lambda_{0})^{-\frac{n\rho}{2}}\exp\left(\rho\lambda_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left\{(1+2P^{\prime}t\chi_{0})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\chi_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}}{1+2P^{\prime}t\chi_{0}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}^{\rho}M^{\rho{t_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{na_{0}}{2}+b_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right),

(156)

where (a) applied the Gaussian identity from Lemma
5
.

\rho
-trick for
\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}}

Given
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
,
we recall that
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}\subset\mathcal{W}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}
and
|\mathcal{W}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}|={\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}=\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}
, hence there are
\binom{\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}}{t}
ways to construct
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
with size
|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t
.
Applying the
\rho
-trick (
111
) again to add a union over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}
to the left side of (
156
)
yields that for
\rho_{1}\in[0,1]
,

term (ii)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}}|=t}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}:|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}}|={\hat{t}}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{e}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{e}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1],\\
\lambda_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}}\end{subarray}}\binom{\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\;\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}},\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}{t_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}a_{0}}{2}+\rho_{1}b_{0}\|c(\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}})-c(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1],\\
\lambda_{0}>-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}}\end{subarray}}\binom{\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}{t_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}a_{0}}{2}\right)(1-2\rho_{1}P_{1}b_{0})^{-\frac{n}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{=}}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho,\rho_{1}\in[0,1],\\
\lambda>-\frac{1}{P^{\prime}{\hat{t}}}\end{subarray}}\binom{\min\{{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}},\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}\}}{t}^{\rho_{1}}\binom{\mathcal{R}}{{t_{\min}}}^{\rho\rho_{1}}M^{\rho\rho_{1}{t_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{n\rho_{1}a}{2}\right)(1-\rho_{1}P_{1}b)^{-\frac{n}{2}}=p(t,{\hat{t}})\ ,

(157)

where (a) applied (
154
); (b) used the shorthand
P_{1}=(\left\lvert\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{iMD}}\right\rvert+|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{iFA}}|)P^{\prime}+1=(({\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}}-\overline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}})^{+}+(\underline{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}-{\kappa_{\mathrm{a}}})^{+})P^{\prime}+1
defined in (
21
); (c) used
\lambda:=2\lambda_{0}
and
a,b,\chi
as defined in (
19
)–(
20
) (thus
a_{0}=a,b_{0}=b/2,\chi_{0}=\chi/2
) and
p(t,{\hat{t}})
as defined in (
14
).
Substituting (
150
) and (
157
) back into (
